And this apperception of reality, Bhagavān, is indeed unreal; therefore the thus-gone one speaks.

?(bhū) 🚺⨀①🆎(bhūta+saṃjñā) ⏏️(iti)

… 'apperception of reality'.

5.15 na me bhagavann āścaryaṃ yad aham imaṃ dharmaparyāyaṃ bhāṣyamāṇam avakalpayāmy adhimucya |  
⏏️(na) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(asmad) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚻⨀①(āścarya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(asmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(idam) 🚹⨀②🆎(dharma+paryāya) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(bhāṣamāṇa) 🟢⨀👆▶️(avakalpayati) 🔽(ya)(adhimucyate)

*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚹⨂①(sattva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(idam) 🚹⨀②🆎(dharma+paryāya) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(udgṛhṇāti)

But indeed, Bhagavān, the apperception of a self will not arise in them.

5.20 na satvasaṃjñā na jīvasaṃjñā na pudgalasaṃjñā |  
⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(sattva+saṃjñā) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(jīva+saṃjñā) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(pudgala+saṃjñā)

5.21 tat kasya hetoḥ sarvasaṃjñāpagatāhi buddhā bhagavantaḥ bhagavān āha |

For what reason? Because the Buddhas, the Bhagavāns have departed from all apperceptions.

5.22 evam etat subhūte paramāścaryasamanvāgatās te bhaviṣyanti ya imaṃ dharmaparyāyaṃ śrutvā nottrasiṣyanti |  
⏏️(evam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(parama+āścarya+samanvāgata) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(idam) 🚹⨀②🆎(dharma+paryāya) 🔽(tvā)(śṛṇoti) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(uttrasati)

5.23 na saṃtrasiṣyanti |  
⏏️(na) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(saṃtrasati)

For what reason is this supreme perfection spoken by the thus-gone one, Subhūti?

5.26 yāṃ ca tathāgataḥ paramapāramitāṃ bhāṣate |

which | and | the thus-gone one | supreme perfection | speaks  
And which supreme perfection the thus-gone one speaks,

5.27 tām aparimāṇā buddhā bhagavanto bhāṣante |

this | immeasurable | Buddhas | Bhagavān | speak  
The immeasurable Buddhas, the Bhagavāns, speak of this.

5.28 tenocyate paramapāramiteti //  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨀①🆎(parama+pāra+mita) ⏏️(iti)  
by that | is called | supreme perfection | thus  
Therefore it is called the supreme perfection.

5.29 api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte ya tathāgatasya kṣāntipāramitā saivāpāramitā |  
⏏️(api) ⏏️(tu) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀⑥(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①🆎(kṣānti+pāramitā) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(saiva+na+pāra+mita)  
but | indeed | now | furthermore | O Subhūti | which | of the thus-gone one | perfection of patience | that very non-perfection  
But furthermore, Subhūti, that which is the thus-gone one's perfection of patience is indeed a non-perfection.

5.30 tat kasya hetoḥ yadā subhūte kalirājāṅgapratyaṃgamāṃsāny acchaitsīt nāsīn me tasmin samaye ātmasaṃjñā vā satvasaṃjñā vā jīvasaṃjñā vā pudgalasaṃjñā vā |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) ⏏️(yadā) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚻⨂①🆎(kali+rāja+aṅga+praty+aṅga+māṃsa) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(chinatti) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟↩️(asti) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(asmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(tad) 🚹⨀⑦(samaya) 🚺⨀①🆎(ātman+saṃjñā) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①🆎(sattva+saṃjñā) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①🆎(jīva+saṃjñā) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①🆎(pudgala+saṃjñā) ⏏️(vā)  
that | of what | cause | when | Subhūti | the flesh of the limbs and members of King Kali | cut off | not | was | in me | in that | time | apperception of a self | or | apperception of a being | or | apperception of a life-force | or | apperception of a person | or  
For what reason? Subhūti, when King Kali cut off the flesh of my limbs and members, at that time I had no apperception of a self, or an apperception of a being, or an apperception of a life-force, or an apperception of a person.

5.31 vyāpādasaṃjñā vāpi me tasmin samaye 'bhaviṣyad abhijānāmy ahaṃ subhūte atīte 'dhvani paṃca jātiśatāni yo 'haṃ kṣāntivādī ṛṣir abhūvaṃs tatrāpi me nātmasaṃjñābhūn na satvasaṃjñā na jīvasaṃjñā na pudgalasaṃjñā |  
🚺⨀①🆎(vyāpāda+saṃjñā) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(api) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(asmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(tad) 🚹⨀⑦(samaya) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) 🟢⨀👆▶️(abhijānāti) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(asmad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(atīta) 🚹⨀⑦(adhvan) 🚻⨂②(pañcan) 🚻⨂②🆎(jāti+śata) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(asmad) 🚹⨀①(kṣāntivādin) 🚹⨀①(ṛṣi) 🟢⨂🤟⏮(bhavati) ⏏️(tatra) ⏏️(api) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(asmad) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(ātman+saṃjñā) 🟢⨀🤟↩️(bhavati) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(sattva+saṃjñā) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(jīva+saṃjñā) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🆎(pudgala+saṃjñā)  
apperception of malice | or | even | in me | in that | time | would have been | I remember | I | Subhūti | in the past | time | five | hundreds of births | who | I | Kṣāntivādin | sage | was | even there | even | in me | not | apperception of a self | was | not | apperception of a being | not | apperception of a life-force | not | apperception of a person  
I remember, Subhūti, that in the past, during five hundred births when I was a sage who preached patience, even then I had no apperception of a self, no apperception of a being, no apperception of a life-force, and no apperception of a person.

5.32 tasmāt tarhi subhūte bodhisatvena mahāsatvena sarvasaṃjñā varjayitvā ...  
⏏️(tasmāt) ⏏️(tarhi) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀③(bodhisattva) 🚹⨀③(mahāsattva) 🚺⨂②🆎(sarva+saṃjñā) 🔽(tvā)(varjati)  
therefore | then | O Subhūti | by a bodhisattva | mahāsattva | all apperceptions | having abandoned  
Therefore, then, Subhūti, a `bodhisattva` (bodhisattva), a great being, having abandoned all apperceptions ...

### §§7

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
> ... All those beings will bear my `aṃsa` (share) of `bodhi` (awakening). For what reason? It is not possible, Subhūti, for this `dharmaparyāya` (method of teaching) to be heard by beings of `hīnādhimuktika` (inferior resolve), nor is it possible for it to be heard, taken up, or mastered by those with an `ātmadṛṣṭika` (view of a self), or a `satva-jīva-pudgala-dṛṣṭika` (view of a being, life-principle, or person). This state of affairs does not exist. But furthermore, Subhūti, whatever spot of earth this `sūtra` shall be proclaimed upon, that spot of earth will be worthy of worship. It will be worthy of veneration and circumambulation by the world with its gods, humans, and asuras. That spot of earth will become like a `caitya` (shrine). Those `kulaputras` (sons of good family) or `kuladuhitṛs` (daughters of good family), Subhūti, who will take up `sūtra` passages of this kind, and master them, they will be despised, utterly despised. And whatever evil `karmas` (actions) of those beings from previous births were `apāyasaṃvartanīyāni` (leading to a lower rebirth), those will be extinguished in this very life through that despisal, and they will attain `buddhabodhi` (the awakening of a Buddha). I recall, Subhūti, in a time past, `asaṃkhyeya` (incalculable) `kalpas` (aeons) ago, more incalculable still, before Dīpaṃkara the `Tathāgata`, `Arhat`, `Samyaksaṃbuddha`, there were eighty-four hundreds of thousands of millions of billions of `Buddhas`, whom I honoured, and having honoured, did not dishonour. And that I, Subhūti, honoured those `Buddhas`, Bhagavāns, and did not dishonour them, and that in the last time, when the final `pañcāśat` (period of five hundred years) is occurring, they will take up these `sūtra` passages, and master them — of this latter `puṇyaskandha` (mass of merit), Subhūti, that former `puṇyaskandha` does not approach even the one-hundredth part, nor the one-thousandth part, nor the one-hundred-thousandth part. It does not bear number, nor fraction, nor counting, nor comparison, nor analogy. Should I, Subhūti, declare the `puṇyaskandha` of those `kulaputras` and `kuladuhitṛs`, to what extent those beings, `kulaputras` and `kuladuhitṛs`, will at that time acquire a `puṇyaskandha`, beings would attain madness or go into mental distraction. But furthermore, Subhūti, this `dharmaparyāya` is inconceivable; its result is likewise inconceivable.

He said:

> How, Bhagavān, should one who has set out in the `bodhisatvayāna` (`bodhisatvayāna`-way) stand, how should one train, how should one control the mind?

Bhagavān said:

> Here, Subhūti, one who has set out in the `bodhisatvayāna` should produce the thought thus: "All beings are to be led by me to final `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) in the `anupadhiśeṣa nirvāṇadhātu` (realm of extinguishment without remaining residue)." And having thus led beings to final `nirvāṇa`, not a single being has been led to final `nirvāṇa`.
</TabItem>

... ḥrimāṇena | sarve te satvā mamāṃsena bodhiṃ dhārayiṣyanti | tat kasya hetoḥ na hi śakyaṃ subhūte ayaṃ dharmaparyāyo hīnādhimuktikaiḥ satvaiḥ śrotuṃ| nātmadṛṣṭikair na satvajīvapudgaladṛṣṭikaiḥ śakyaṃ śrotuṃ udgrahītuṃ vā | yāvat paryavāptuṃ vā nedaṃ sthānaṃ vidyate | api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte yatra pṛthivīpradeśe idaṃ sūtraṃ prakāśayiṣyati | pūjanīyaḥ sa pṛthivīpradeśo bhaviṣyati | sadevamānuṣāsurasya lokasya vandanīyaḥ pradakṣiṇīkaraṇīyaś caityabhūta sa pṛthivīpradeśo bhaviṣyati | ye te subhūte kulaputrā vā kuladuhitaro vā | imān evaṃrūpān sūtrāntān udgrahīṣyanti yāvat paryavāpsyanti | te paribhūtā bhaviṣyanti suparibhūtāḥ yāni ca teṣāṃ satvānāṃ pūrvajanmikāny aśubhāni karmāṇy apāyasaṃvartanīyāni tāni dṛṣṭa eva dharme paribhūtatayā kṣapayiṣyanti buddabodhiṃ cānuprāpsyanti | abhijānāmy ahaṃ subhūte atīte 'dhvany asaṃkhyeyaiḥ kalpair asaṃkhyeyatarair dīpaṃkarasya tathāgatasyārhataḥ samyaksaṃbuddhasya pareṇa caturaśītir buddhakoṭīniyutaśatasahasrāṇy abhūvan yāni mayā ārāgitāni ārāgya ca na virāgitāni | yac ca mayā subhūte te buddhā bhagavanta ārāgya na virāgitā

yac ca carime kāle paścimāyāṃ paṃcāśatyāṃ vartamānāyām imāṃ sūtrāntān udgrahīṣyanti | yāvat paryavāpsyanti | asya subhūte puṇyaskandhasyāsau pūrvakaḥ puṇyaskandhaḥ śatatamīm api kalān nopaiti | sahasratamīm api | śatasahasratamīm api | saṃkhyām api kalām api gaṇanām apy upamām apy upaniśām api na kṣamate | sacet subhūte teṣāṃ kulaputrāṇāṃ kuladuhitrīṇāṃ ca puṇyaskandhaṃ bhāṣeyaṃ yāvantas te satvā kulaputrāḥ kuladuhitaraś ca tasmin samaye puṇyaskandhaṃ parigrahīṣyanti | unmādaṃ satvā anuprāpnuyuś cittavikṣepaṃ vā gaccheyuḥ api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte acintyo 'yaṃ dharmaparyāyaḥ asyācintya eva vipākaḥ //

āha | kathaṃ bhagavan bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitena sthātavyaṃ kathaṃ pratipattavyaṃ kathaṃ cittaṃ pragṛhītavyaṃ | bhagavān āha | iha subhūte bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitenaivaṃ cittam utpādayitavyaṃ sarvasatvā mayā anupadhiśeṣe nirvāṇadhātau parinirvāpayitavyāḥ evaṃ ca satvān parinirvāpya na kaścit satvaḥ parinirvāpito bhavati |

7.1 ḥrimāṇena |  
?(ḥrimāṇena)  
(fragment)  
… (fragment).

7.2 sarve te satvā mamāṃsena bodhiṃ dhārayiṣyanti |  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(sarva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①(sattva) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(asmad) 🚹⨀③(aṃsa) 🚺⨀②(bodhi) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(dhārayati)  
all | those | beings | my | shoulder | awakening | will bear  
All those beings will bear my `bodhi` (awakening) on their shoulders.

7.3 tat kasya hetoḥ na hi śakyaṃ subhūte ayaṃ dharmaparyāyo hīnādhimuktikaiḥ satvaiḥ śrotuṃ|  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) 🚻⨀①🔽(ya)(śakya) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(idam) 🚹⨀①🆎(dharma+paryāya) 🚹⨂③🆎(hīna+adhimuktika) 🚹⨂③(sattva) 🔽(tum)(śṛṇoti)  
that | of what | cause | not | indeed | possible | Subhūti | this | method of teaching | by those of inferior resolve | beings | to hear  
For what reason? Because, Subhūti, it is not possible for `sattva` (beings) of inferior resolve to hear this method of teaching.

7.4 nātmadṛṣṭikair na satvajīvapudgaladṛṣṭikaiḥ śakyaṃ śrotuṃ udgrahītuṃ vā |  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨂③🆎(ātman+dṛṣṭika) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂③🆎(sattva+jīva+pudgala+dṛṣṭika) 🚻⨀①🔽(ya)(śakya) 🔽(tum)(śṛṇoti) 🔽(tum)(udgṛhṇāti) ⏏️(vā)  
not | by those who hold the view of a self | not | by those who hold the view of a life-force, a life-force, or a person | possible | to hear | or to grasp | or  
It is not possible for those who hold the view of a self, nor for those who hold the view of a life-force, a life-force, or a person, to hear or grasp it.

7.5 yāvat paryavāptuṃ vā nedaṃ sthānaṃ vidyate |  
⏏️(yāvat) 🚻⨀⑤(paryavāpti) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(ned) 🚻⨀①(sthāna) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
as long as | to master | or | not | this | opportunity  
As long as there is no opportunity to master this, or even to hear it.

7.6 api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte yatra pṛthivīpradeśe idaṃ sūtraṃ prakāśayiṣyati |  
⏏️(api) ⏏️(tu) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(yatra) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(pṛthivī+pradeśa) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) 🚻⨀①(sūtra) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(prakāśayati)  
even | but | now | again | O Subhūti | wherever | on a spot of earth | this | discourse | will be revealed  
But indeed, Subhūti, on whatever spot of earth this discourse will be revealed,

7.7 pūjanīyaḥ sa pṛthivīpradeśo bhaviṣyati |  
🚹⨀①🔽(anīya)(pūjanīya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①🆎(pṛthivī+pradeśa) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
to be honoured | that | region of earth | will be  
That region of earth will be worthy of worship.

7.8 sadevamānuṣāsurasya lokasya vandanīyaḥ pradakṣiṇīkaraṇīyaś caityabhūta sa pṛthivīpradeśo bhaviṣyati |  
🚹⨀⑥🆎(sa+deva+mānuṣa+asura) 🚹⨀⑥(loka) 🚹⨀①🔽(anīya)(vandanīya) 🚹⨀①🔽(anīya)🆎(pradakṣiṇa+karaṇīya) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(caitya+bhūta) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①🆎(pṛthivī+pradeśa) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
with its gods, humans and asuras | of the world | to be saluted | to be circumambulated | become a shrine | that | region of earth | will be  
That region of earth will become a shrine, to be saluted and circumambulated by the world including gods, humans, and asuras.

7.9 ye te subhūte kulaputrā vā kuladuhitaro vā |  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨂①🆎(kula+putra) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨂①🆎(kula+duhitṛ) ⏏️(vā)  
who | those | Subhūti | sons of good family | or | daughters of good family | or  
Those sons of good family or daughters of good family, Subhūti,

7.10 imān evaṃrūpān sūtrāntān udgrahīṣyanti yāvat paryavāpsyanti |  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(idam) 🚹⨂②🆎(evaṃ+rūpa) 🚹⨂②(sūtrānta) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(udgṛhṇāti) ⏏️(yāvat) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(paryavāpnoti)  
these | of such a form | discourses | will take up | up to | will master  
They will take up these discourses of such a form, and so on, until they master them.

7.11 te paribhūtā bhaviṣyanti suparibhūtāḥ yāni ca teṣāṃ satvānāṃ pūrvajanmikāny aśubhāni karmāṇy apāyasaṃvartanīyāni tāni dṛṣṭa eva dharme paribhūtatayā kṣapayiṣyanti buddabodhiṃ cānuprāpsyanti |  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(paribhūta) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(su+paribhūta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨂①(yad) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(tad) 🚹⨂⑥(sattva) 🚻⨂①🆎(pūrva+janmika) 🚻⨂①🆎(na+śubha) 🚻⨂①(karman) 🚻⨂①🆎(apāya+saṃvartanīya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(dṛṣṭa) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑦(dharma) 🚺⨀③🔽(tā)(paribhūtatā) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(kṣapayati) 🚺⨀②🆎(buddha+bodhi) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(anuprāpnoti)  
they | despised | will be | greatly despised | which | and | of them | of the beings | previous-life | unpleasant | actions | leading to the lower realms | those | in this present | very | life | by being humiliated | will exhaust | the awakening of a Buddha | and | will attain  
They will be humiliated and greatly humiliated, and those unpleasant `karman` (actions) of those beings from previous lives which lead to the lower realms will be exhausted in this very life through that humiliation, and they will attain the awakening of a Buddha.

7.12 abhijānāmy ahaṃ subhūte atīte 'dhvany asaṃkhyeyaiḥ kalpair asaṃkhyeyatarair dīpaṃkarasya tathāgatasyārhataḥ samyaksaṃbuddhasya pareṇa caturaśītir buddhakoṭīniyutaśatasahasrāṇy abhūvan yāni mayā ārāgitāni ārāgya ca na virāgitāni |  
🟢⨀👆▶️(abhijānāti) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(asmad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(atīta) 🚹⨀⑦(adhvan) 🚹⨂③🔽(ya)🆎(na+saṃkhyeya) 🚹⨂③(kalpa) 🚹⨂③🔽(tara)🆎(na+saṃkhyeya) 🚹⨀⑥(dīpaṃkara) 🚹⨀⑥(tathāgata) 🚹⨀⑥(arhat) 🚹⨀⑥(samyaksaṃbuddha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(para) 🚺⨀①🆎(catur+aśīti) 🚻⨂①🆎(buddha+koṭī+niyuta+śata+sahasra) 🟢⨂🤟⏮(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨂①(yad) *️⃣👆⚧⨀③(asmad) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(ārāgita) 🔽(ya)(ārāgayati) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(virāgita)  
I remember | I | Subhūti | in the past | time | innumerable | aeons | even more innumerable | of Dīpaṃkara | of the thus-gone one | of the liberated one | Samyaksaṃbuddha | beyond | eighty-four | hundreds of thousands of millions of billions of Buddhas | there were | which | by me | honoured | having honoured | and | not | disrespected  
I remember, Subhūti, in the past, innumerable and even more innumerable aeons ago, that there were eighty-four hundred thousand million billion Buddhas beyond the time of the thus-gone one, worthy one, Samyaksaṃbuddha Dīpaṃkara, whom I pleased and did not displease.

7.13 yac ca mayā subhūte te buddhā bhagavanta ārāgya na virāgitā  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣👆⚧⨀③(asmad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①(buddha) 🚹⨂①(bhagavat) 🔽(ya)(ārāgayati) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(virāgita)  
which | and | by me | Subhūti | those | Buddhas | Bhagavāns | having honoured | not | disrespected  
And which those Buddhas, the Bhagavāns, were pleased by me and not displeased, Subhūti.

7.14 yac ca carime kāle paścimāyāṃ paṃcāśatyāṃ vartamānāyām imāṃ sūtrāntān udgrahīṣyanti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⑦(carima) 🚻⨀⑦(kāla) 🚺⨀⑦(paścima) 🚺⨀⑦(pañcāśati) 🚺⨀⑦🔽(māna)(vartamāna) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀②(idam) 🚹⨂②(sūtrānta) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(udgṛhṇāti)  
which | and | in the last | time | in the final | five-hundred-year period | present | these | discourses | will take up  
and which, in the final time, during the last five-hundred-year period, will take up these discourses.

7.15 yāvat paryavāpsyanti |  
⏏️(yāvat) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(paryavāpnoti)  
as long as | they will master  
as long as they will master it.

7.16 asya subhūte puṇyaskandhasyāsau pūrvakaḥ puṇyaskandhaḥ śatatamīm api kalān nopaiti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀⑥(idam) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(puṇya+skandha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(adas) 🚹⨀①(pūrvaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🚺⨀②🆎(śata+tami) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨂②(kala) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(upaiti)  
of this | O Subhūti | mass of merit | this | former | mass of merit | hundredth | even | part | not | approaches  
O Subhūti, this former `puṇyaskandha` (mass of merit) does not approach even a hundredth part of this mass of merit.

7.17 sahasratamīm api |  
🚺⨀②🆎(sahasra+tami) ⏏️(api)  
a thousandth part | even  
even a thousandth part.

7.18 śatasahasratamīm api |  
🚺⨀②🆎(śata+sahasra+tami) ⏏️(api)  
a hundred-thousandth part | even  
even a hundred-thousandth part.

7.19 saṃkhyām api kalām api gaṇanām apy upamām apy upaniśām api na kṣamate |  
🚺⨀②(saṃkhyā) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀②(kala) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀②(gaṇanā) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(upamām) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀②(upaniśā) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kṣamate)  
number | even | a part | even | calculation | even | comparison | even | likeness | even | not | is able to bear  
It does not even bear comparison, nor a part, nor a calculation, nor a likeness, nor a resemblance.

7.20 sacet subhūte teṣāṃ kulaputrāṇāṃ kuladuhitrīṇāṃ ca puṇyaskandhaṃ bhāṣeyaṃ yāvantas te satvā kulaputrāḥ kuladuhitaraś ca tasmin samaye puṇyaskandhaṃ parigrahīṣyanti |  
⏏️(sacet) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(tad) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(kula+putra) 🚺⨂⑥🆎(kula+duhitṛ) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🚺⨀①(bhāṣā) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(idam) 🚹⨂①(yāvat) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①(sattva) 🚹⨂①🆎(kula+putra) 🚺⨂①🆎(kula+duhitṛ) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(tad) 🚹⨀⑦(samaya) 🚹⨀②🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(parigṛhṇāti)  
if | Subhūti | of them | sons of good family | daughters of good family | and | mass of merit | I were to speak | known, present | as many as | those | beings | sons of good family | daughters of good family | and | at that | time | mass of merit | will acquire  
If, Subhūti, I were to speak of the mass of merit that those sons and daughters of good family will acquire at that time, it would be impossible to describe.

7.21 unmādaṃ satvā anuprāpnuyuś cittavikṣepaṃ vā gaccheyuḥ api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte acintyo 'yaṃ dharmaparyāyaḥ asyācintya eva vipākaḥ //  
🚻⨀②(unmāda) 🚹⨂①(sattva) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(anuprāpnoti) 🚹⨀②🆎(citta+vikṣepa) ⏏️(vā) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(gacchati) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(tu) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀①🔽(ya)🆎(na+cintya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(idam) 🚹⨀①🆎(dharma+paryāya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(idam) 🚹⨀①🔽(ya)🆎(na+cintya) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀①(vipāka)  
madness | beings | would attain | mental distraction | or | would go | even | but | indeed | furthermore | O Subhūti | inconceivable | this | method of teaching | of this | inconceivable | only | maturation  
Beings might attain insanity or undergo mental distraction; however, Subhūti, this method of teaching is indeed inconceivable, and its `vipāka` (maturation) is also inconceivable.

7.22 āha |  
🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
said  
He said:

7.23 kathaṃ bhagavan bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitena sthātavyaṃ kathaṃ pratipattavyaṃ kathaṃ cittaṃ pragṛhītavyaṃ |  
⏏️(katham) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)🆎(bodhi+sattva+yāna+saṃprasthita) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(sthātavya) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(pratipattavya) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①(citta) 🚻⨀①🆎(pra+gṛhītavya)  
how | Bhagavān | by one who has set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas | should stand | how | should practice | how | the mind | should be uplifted  
How, Bhagavān, should one who has set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas stand, how should they practice, and how should they uplift their `citta` (mind)?

7.24 bhagavān āha |  
🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
the Bhagavān | said  
The Bhagavān said:

7.25 iha subhūte bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitenaivaṃ cittam utpādayitavyaṃ sarvasatvā mayā anupadhiśeṣe nirvāṇadhātau parinirvāpayitavyāḥ evaṃ ca satvān parinirvāpya na kaścit satvaḥ parinirvāpito bhavati |  
⏏️(iha) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)🆎(bodhisattva+yāna+saṃprasthita) ⏏️(evam) 🚻⨀①(citta) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(utpādayitavya) 🚹⨂①🆎(sarva+sattva) *️⃣👆⚧⨀③(asmad) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(na+upadhi+śeṣa) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(nirvāṇa+dhātu) 🚹⨂①🔽(tavya)(parinirvāpayitavya) ⏏️(evam) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂②(sattva) 🔽(ya)(parinirvāpayati) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(sattva) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(parinirvāpita) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati)  
here | O Subhūti | by one who has set out in the bodhisattva vehicle | thus | mind | should be produced | all beings | by me | in the extinguishment without remainder of the components | in the region of nirvāṇa | should be completely extinguished | thus | and | beings | having led to final extinguishment | not | any | being | led to final extinguishment | is  
Here, O Subhūti, one who has set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas should produce this mind: 'All beings must be completely extinguished by me in the region of nirvāṇa without remainder of the components'; and yet, having thus completely extinguished existences, not a single being is actually extinguished.

### §§8

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
> For what reason? If, Subhūti, a `satvasaṃjñā` (apperception of a being), `jīvasaṃjñā` (apperception of a life-principle), or `pudgalasaṃjñā` (apperception of a person) should operate in a `bodhisattva` (awakening-being), he should not be called a '`bodhisattva`'. For what reason? There is not, Subhūti, any `dharma` (phenomenon) which is named 'one who has set out in the `bodhisatvayāna` (`bodhisattva`-way)'. What do you think, Subhūti, is there any `dharma`  by which the `Tathāgata` fully awakened to `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` (unexcelled perfect and complete awakening) in the presence of the Dīpaṃkara `Tathāgata`?

He said:

> There is no `dharma`, Bhagavān, by which the `Tathāgata` fully awakened to `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` in the presence of the Dīpaṃkara `Tathāgata`.

Bhagavān said:

> Therefore I was prophesied by the Dīpaṃkara `Tathāgata`: "You, young man, in a future time, will be a `Tathāgata`, `Arhat`, `Samyaksaṃbuddha`, named Śākyamuni." For what reason? '`Tathāgata`', Subhūti, is an epithet for `tathatā` (suchness). Whoever, Subhūti, should say thus: "The `Tathāgata` has fully awakened to `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi`" — there is no `dharma`, Subhūti, that has been fully awakened to as `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` by the `Tathāgata`. The `dharma`, Subhūti, that was fully awakened to by the `Tathāgata`, therein is neither truth nor falsehood. Therefore the `Tathāgata` says: "All `dharmas` are `buddhadharmas` (phenomena of a Buddha)." 'All `dharmas`', Subhūti, all of them are non-`dharmas`; therefore they are called 'all `dharmas`'.
>
> Just as, for example, Subhūti, there might be a man with a large body, a great body.

Subhūti said:

> That which was spoken of by the `Tathāgata` as a man with a large body, a great body — a non-body, Bhagavān, was spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore he is called 'a man with a large body, a great body'.

Bhagavān said:

> So it is, Subhūti. A `bodhisattva` who would say thus: "I shall lead beings to final `nirvāṇa`" should not be called a '`bodhisattva`'. For what reason? Is there, Subhūti, any `dharma` that is called a '`bodhisattva`'

He said:

> There is not, Bhagavān.

Bhagavān said:

> Therefore the `Tathāgata` says: "All `dharmas` are without being, without life-principle, without person." A `bodhisattva`, Subhūti, who would say thus: "I shall accomplish the `kṣetravyūhas` (literally: arrangements of a field, but presumably an epithet for "establishing a buddha-field")," he too should be spoken of in the same way. For what reason? '`Kṣetravyūhas`, `kṣetravyūhas`, Subhūti, are non-arrangements spoken of by the `Tathāgata`; therefore they are called '`kṣetravyūhas`'. That `bodhisattva`, Subhūti, who is convinced "`dharmas` are selfless, `dharmas` are selfless," is declared by the `Tathāgata`, the `Arhat`, the `Samyaksaṃbuddha` as '`bodhisattva`, `bodhisattva`'. What do you think, Subhūti, does the `Tathāgata` possess the `māṃsacakṣuḥ` (physical eye)?

He said:

> So it is, Bhagavān, the `Tathāgata` possesses the `māṃsacakṣuḥ` (physical eye).
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
tat kasya hetoḥ sacet subhūte bodhisatvasya satvasaṃjñā pravarteta | jīvasaṃjñā pudgalasaṃjñā vā na sa bodhisatva iti vaktavyaḥ tat kasya hetoḥ nāsti subhūte sa dharmo yo bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthito nāma | tat kiṃ manyase subhūte asti sa kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatena dīpaṃkarasya tathāgatasyāntikād anuttarāṃ samyaksaṃbodhim abhisaṃbuddhaḥ āha | nāsti sa bhagavan kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatena dīpaṃkarasya tathāgatasyāntikād anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhim abhisaṃbuddhaḥ āha | tasmād ahaṃ dīpaṃkareṇa tathāgatena vyākṛto bhaviṣyasi tvaṃ māṇavānāgate 'dhvani śākyamunir nāma tathāgato 'rhan samyaksaṃbuddhas tat kasya hetos tathāgata iti subhūte tathatāyā etad adhivacanaṃ yaḥ kaścit subhūte evaṃ vadet tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddheti | nāsti subhūte sa kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhaḥ yaḥ subhūte tathāgatena dharmo 'bhisaṃbuddhas tatra na satyaṃ na mṛṣāḥ tasmāt tathāgato bhāṣate | sarvadharmā buddhadharmā iti | sarvadharmā iti subhūte sarve te adharmās tenocyante sarvadharmā iti //

He should not be called a bodhisattva if he has the apperception of a life-force or the apperception of a person; for what reason? O Subhūti, there is no such dharma as one who has set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas.

therefore | I | by Dīpaṃkara | by the thus-gone one | was predicted | you will be | you | young man | in the future | in the future time | Śākyamuni | by name | a thus-gone one | a liberated one | a Samyaksaṃbuddha | that | of what | cause | thus-gone one | thus | O Subhūti | of suchness | this | designation | whoever | anyone | O Subhūti | thus | should say | by the thus-gone one | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | fully awakened | thus  
Therefore I was predicted by the thus-gone one Dīpaṃkara: 'You, young man, will in a future time become the thus-gone one, the `arhat` (liberated one), the Samyaksaṃbuddha named Śākyamuni.' For what reason? 'Thus-gone one,' Subhūti, is a designation for suchness. Whoever, Subhūti, should say: 'The unsurpassed `samyaksaṃbodhi` (perfect awakening) was `abhisaṃbuddha` (fully awakened) to by the thus-gone one'—

Subhūti, there is no dharma whatsoever which was fully awakened to by the thus-gone one as unsurpassed perfect awakening; and that dharma which was fully awakened to by the thus-gone one, therein there is neither `satya` (truth) nor falsehood; therefore the thus-gone one speaks.

This man was spoken of by the thus-gone one as being endowed with a body and large-bodied; he, Bhagavān, was spoken of by the thus-gone one as bodiless, and therefore he is called 'endowed with a body' and 'large-bodied'; the Bhagavān said:

He too should be spoken of in the same way. For what reason? Subhūti, field-arrays are field-arrays; they have been spoken of by the thus-gone one as non-arrays, and therefore they are called field-arrays.

Subhūti, if a bodhisattva is convinced that dharmas are selfless and dharmas are selfless, he is called a bodhisattva by the thus-gone one, the liberated one, the Samyaksaṃbuddha. What do you think, Subhūti, does the thus-gone one have a physical eye?

Bhagavān, it is so: the thus-gone one possesses a physical eye.

### §§9

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
Bhagavān said:

> What do you think, Subhūti, does the `Tathāgata` possess the `divyaṃ cakṣuḥ` (divine eye), the `prajñācakṣuḥ` (wisdom eye), the `dharmacakṣuḥ` (Dharma eye), the `buddhacakṣuḥ` (Buddha eye)?

He said:

> So it is, Bhagavān, the `Tathāgata` possesses the `divyaṃ cakṣuḥ`, the `prajñācakṣuḥ`, the `dharmacakṣuḥ`, the `buddhacakṣuḥ`.

Bhagavān said:

> What do you think, Subhūti, if there were as many Ganges rivers as there are grains of sand in the river Ganges, and as many `lokadhātus` (world-systems) as the grains of sand in them, would those `lokadhātus` (world-systems) be many?

Bhagavān said:
> As many beings as there are, Subhūti, in those `lokadhātus` (world-systems), I would know their various streams of thought. For what reason? '`Cittadhārā` (stream of thought)', '`cittadhārā`', Subhūti, are non-streams spoken of by the `Tathāgata`; therefore they are called '`cittadhārā`'. For what reason? Past thought, Subhūti, is not apprehended. Future thought is not apprehended. Present thought is not apprehended. What do you think, Subhūti, if a `kulaputra` (son of good family) or `kuladuhitṛ` (daughter of good family), having filled this three-thousand-great-thousand `lokadhātu` with the seven precious things, were to give a gift, would he or she on that account generate much `puṇya` (merit)?

He said:

> Much, Bhagavān, much, Sugata.

Bhagavān said:

> So it is, Subhūti, so it is. That `kulaputra` or `kuladuhitṛ` would on that account generate much `puṇya`. If, Subhūti, a `puṇyaskandha` (mass of merit) existed, the `Tathāgata` would not have said, '`puṇyaskandha`, `puṇyaskandha`'. What do you think, Subhūti, is the `Tathāgata` to be seen by the perfection of the `rūpakāya` (form-body)?

He said:

> No, Bhagavān, the `Tathāgata` is not to be seen by the perfection of the `rūpakāya`. For what reason? 'Perfection of the `rūpakāya`', 'perfection of the `rūpakāya`', this is a no-perfection spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore it is called 'perfection of the `rūpakāya`'.

Bhagavān said:
> What do you think, Subhūti, is the `Tathāgata` to be seen by the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad` (perfection of marks)?

He said:

> No, Bhagavān, the `Tathāgata` is not to be seen by the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad` (perfection of marks). For what reason? That which is the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad` (perfection of marks) spoken of by the `Tathāgata`, this is a no-perfection of marks spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore it is called '`lakṣaṇasaṃpad` (perfection of marks)'.

Bhagavān said:

> What do you think, Subhūti, does it occur to the `Tathāgata` thus: "A `dharma` (teaching) has not been taught by me"? Whoever, Subhūti, should say thus: "A `dharma` has been taught by the `Tathāgata`," he would be slandering me, Subhūti, with what is grasped as untrue. For what reason? 'Discourse on the `Dharma`', 'discourse on the `Dharma`', Subhūti, there is no `dharma` (phenomenon) that is found which is named 'discourse on the `Dharma`'.

He said:

> Are there, Bhagavān, any beings who will exist in a future time, who, upon hearing `dharmas` of this kind being spoken, will have faith?

Bhagavān said:

> They, Subhūti, are not beings, nor non-beings. For what reason? 'All beings', Subhūti, are non-beings spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore they are called 'all beings'.
</TabItem>

Then what do you think, Subhūti, does the thus-gone one possess a supernatural eye, an eye of wisdom, a `dharmacakṣus` (Dharma eye), and an eye of a Buddha? He said: 'It is so, Bhagavān, the thus-gone one possesses a supernatural eye, an eye of wisdom, a Dharma eye, and an eye of a Buddha.'

Subhūti, as many beings as there are in those world systems, I know their various streams of mind; why is that? Subhūti, 'streams of mind, streams of mind' are spoken of by the thus-gone one as non-streams, and therefore they are called 'streams of mind'.

The present is not perceived.

9.9 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte ya imaṃ trisāhasramahāsāhasraṃ lokadhātuṃ saptaratnaparipūrṇaṃ kṛtvā dānaṃ dadyād api nu sa kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā tatonidānaṃ bahu puṇyaṃ prasaveta |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(idam) 🚻⨀①🆎(tri+sāhasra+mahā+sāhasra) 🚹⨀②🆎(loka+dhātu) 🚻⨀①🆎(saptaratna+paripūrṇa) 🔽(tvā)(karoti) 🚻⨀①(dāna) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(dadāti) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①🆎(kula+putra) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①🆎(kula+duhitṛ) ⏏️(vā) 🚻⨀①🆎(tatas+nidāna) ⏏️(bahu) 🚻⨀①(puṇya) 🟢⨂🤘⏯(prasavati)  
then | what | do you think | Subhūti | who | this | three-thousand-great-thousand world system | world system | filled with the seven jewels | having made | gift | should give | even | now | he | son of good family | or | daughter of good family | or | on that account | much | merit | would produce  
What do you think, Subhūti, if a son of good family or a daughter of good family were to fill this three-thousand-great-thousand world system with the seven jewels and give it as a `dāna` (gift), would he or she produce much merit on that account?

9.10 āha |  
🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
said  
He said:

9.11 bahu bhagavan bahu sugata |  
🚻⨀①(bahu) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚻⨀①(bahu) 🚹⨀⓪(sugata)  
much | Bhagavān | much | Sugata  
Much, Bhagavān, much, Sugata.

9.12 bhagavān āha |  
🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
the Bhagavān | said  
The Bhagavān said:

9.13 evam etat subhūte evam etad bahu sa kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā tatonidānaṃ bahu puṇyaṃ prasaveta |  
⏏️(evam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(evam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🚻⨀①(bahu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①🆎(kula+putra) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①🆎(kula+duhitṛ) ⏏️(vā) 🚻⨀①🆎(tatas+nidāna) 🚻⨀①(bahu) 🚻⨀②(puṇya) 🟢⨂🤘⏯(prasavati)  
thus | this | Subhūti | thus | this | much | he | son of a noble family | or | daughter of a noble family | or | on that account | much | merit | would produce  
Subhūti, it is so; it is so. That son of a noble family or daughter of a noble family would produce much merit on that account.

9.14 sacet subhūte puṇyaskandho 'bhaviṣyan na tathāgato 'bhāṣiṣyat puṇyaskandhaḥ puṇyaskandha iti |  
⏏️(sacet) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🔵⨀🤟🔀(bhāṣate) 🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) ⏏️(iti)  
if | Subhūti | mass of merit | had existed | not | the thus-gone one | would have spoken | mass of merit | mass of merit | thus  
Subhūti, if the mass of merit had existed, the thus-gone one would not have spoken of it as 'a mass of merit, a mass of merit'.

9.15 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte rūpakāyapariniṣpattyā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚺⨀③🆎(rūpaka+pariniṣpatti) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya)  
that | what | do you think | O Subhūti | by the perfection of the form-body | the thus-gone one | to be seen  
What do you think, Subhūti, is the thus-gone one to be seen by the perfection of the form-body?

9.16 āha |  
🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
said  
He said:

⏏️(no) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀③🆎(rūpaka+pariniṣpatti) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) 🚺⨁①🆎(rūpaka+pariniṣpatti) 🚺⨀①🆎(rūpaka+pariniṣpatti) ⏏️(iti) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+pariniṣpatti) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚺⨀①🆎(rūpaka+pariniṣpatti) ⏏️(iti)  
not | Bhagavān | not | by the perfection of the form-body | the thus-gone one | to be seen | that | of what | cause | the perfection of the form-body | the perfection of the form-body | thus | non-perfection | this | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | is called | the perfection of the form-body | thus  
No, Bhagavān. The thus-gone one is not to be seen by the perfection of the form-body. Why is that? The perfection of the form-body, the perfection of the form-body—this was spoken by the thus-gone one as non-perfection. Therefore it is called 'the perfection of the form-body'.

*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)

9.20 no bhagavan na lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ tat kasya hetoḥ yaiṣā lakṣaṇasaṃpat tathāgatena bhāṣitā alakṣaṇasaṃpad eṣā tathāgatena bhāṣitā tenocyate lakṣaṇasaṃpad iti |  
⏏️(no) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) 🚺⨀①(yā) 🚺⨀①(eṣā) 🚺⨀①🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①(bhāṣita) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚺⨀①(eṣā) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①(bhāṣita) ⏏️(tena) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚺⨀①🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) ⏏️(iti)  
not | Bhagavān | not | by the perfection of physical characteristics | the thus-gone one | to be seen | that | of what | reason | which | this | perfection of physical characteristics | by the thus-gone one | spoken | perfection of non-characteristics | this | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | is called | perfection of physical characteristics | thus  
No, Bhagavān. The thus-gone one is not to be seen by the perfection of physical characteristics. Why is that? This perfection of physical characteristics which was spoken of by the thus-gone one was spoken of by the thus-gone one as a perfection of non-characteristics; therefore it is called the perfection of physical characteristics.

🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
the Bhagavān | said  
The Bhagavān said:

9.22 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte api nu tathāgatasyaivaṃ bhavati na mayā dharmo deśita iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nu) 🚹⨀⑥(tathāgata) ⏏️(evam) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati) ⏏️(na) *️⃣👆⚧⨀③(asmad) 🚹⨀①(dharma) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(deśita) ⏏️(iti)  
then | what | do you think | Subhūti | even | indeed | of the thus-gone one | thus | it occurs | not | by me | the Dharma | taught | thus  
Then what do you think, Subhūti? Does it occur to the thus-gone one that 'the Dharma has been taught by me'?

9.23 yaḥ subhūte evaṃ vadet tathāgatena dharmo deśita iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(evam) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(vadati) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①(dharma) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(deśita) ⏏️(iti)  
who | Subhūti | thus | should say | by the thus-gone one | the Dharma | has been taught | thus  
Subhūti, whoever should say, 'The Dharma has been taught by the thus-gone one,'

9.24 abhyācakṣīta māṃsa subhūte asatād udgṛhītena |  
🔵⨀🤟⏯(abhyācaṣṭe) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(asmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀③🔽(at)(asat) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(udgṛhīta)  
would slander | me | he | Subhūti | by what is untrue | by what is grasped  
Subhūti, he would falsely accuse me by grasping what is unreal.

9.25 tat kasya hetor dharmadeśanā dharmadeśaneti subhūte nāsti sa kaścid dharmo yo dharmadeśanā nāmopalabhyate |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) 🚺⨀①🆎(dharma+deśanā) 🚺⨀①🆎(dharma+deśanā) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(nāsti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(dharma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚺⨀①🆎(dharma+deśanā) ⏏️(nāma) 🔴⨀🤟▶️(upalabhyate)  
that | of what | cause | teaching of the Dharma | teaching of the Dharma | thus | Subhūti | there is not | that | any | dharma | which | teaching of the Dharma | by name | is found  
For what reason is it called the teaching of the Dharma, Subhūti? There is no such dharma whatsoever that can be found by the name 'teaching of the Dharma'.

9.26 āhāsti bhagavan kecit satvā bhaviṣyanty anāgate 'dhvani ya imān evaṃrūpān dharmān bhāṣyamāṇāṃ cchrutvābhiśraddadhāsyanti |  
🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(kaścit) 🚹⨂①(sattva) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)🆎(na+āgata) 🚹⨀⑦(adhvan) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(idam) 🚹⨂②🆎(evaṃ+rūpa) 🚹⨂②(dharma) 🚺⨀②🔽(māna)(bhāṣamāṇa) 🔽(tvā)(śṛṇoti) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(abhiśraddadhāti)  
said | is | Bhagavān | some | beings | will be | in the future | time | who | these | such | teachings | being spoken | having heard | will have faith  
He said: "Bhagavān, will there be some beings in the future time who, having heard these teachings being spoken, will have faith?"

9.27 bhagavān āha |  
🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
the Bhagavān | said  
The Bhagavān said:

9.28 na te subhūte satvā nāsatvās tat kasya hetoḥ sarvasatvā iti subhūte asatvās te tathāgatena bhāṣitās tenocyante sarvasatvā iti |  
⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨂①(sattva) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+sattva) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) 🚹⨂①🆎(sarva+sattva) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+sattva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨂①🆎(sarva+sattva) ⏏️(iti)  
not | they | Subhūti | beings | not | non-beings | that | of what | cause | all beings | thus | Subhūti | non-beings | they | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | are called | all beings | thus  
Subhūti, they are not beings, nor are they non-beings; for what reason? Subhūti, all beings are spoken of by the thus-gone one as non-beings, and therefore they are called 'all beings'.

### §§10

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
> What do you think, Subhūti, is there any `dharma` (phenomenon) that was fully awakened to by the `Tathāgata` as `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` (unexcelled perfect and complete awakening)?

He said:

> There is no `dharma`, Bhagavān, that was fully awakened to by the `Tathāgata` as `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi`.

Bhagavān said:

> So it is, Subhūti, so it is. Not even a minute `dharma` exists or is found there. Therefore it is called '`anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi`'. But furthermore, Subhūti, that `dharma` is equal; there is nothing unequal in it. Therefore it is called '`anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi`'. Through being without life-principle, without being, without person, that same `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` is fully awakened to through all wholesome `dharmas`. 'Wholesome `dharmas`', 'wholesome `dharmas`', Subhūti, they are indeed non-`dharmas` spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore they are called 'wholesome `dharmas`'. And furthermore, Subhūti, if someone, having collected heaps of the seven precious things as numerous as the Sumerus, kings of mountains, in the three-thousand-great-thousand `lokadhātu` (world-system), were to give a gift, and if someone, having learned from this `Prajñāpāramitā` at least a four-lined verse, should teach it to others — of this `puṇyaskandha` (mass of merit), Subhūti, that former `puṇyaskandha` does not approach even the one-hundredth part, as far as not bearing even an analogy.
>
> What do you think, Subhūti, does it occur to the `Tathāgata` thus: "Beings have been liberated by me"? Not so, indeed, Subhūti, should it be seen. For what reason? There is no being who has been liberated by the `Tathāgata`. If again, Subhūti, there had been any being who was liberated by the `Tathāgata`, that itself would be for him a grasp of a self, a grasp of a being, a grasp of a life-principle, a grasp of a person. 'Grasp of a self', Subhūti, is a non-grasp spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. And it is grasped by `bālapṛthagjanas` (foolish common people). '`Bālapṛthagjanas`', Subhūti, are no-people spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore they are called '`bālapṛthagjanas`'. What do you think, Subhūti, is the `Tathāgata` to be seen by the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad` (perfection of marks)?

He said:

> So it is, Bhagavān, the `Tathāgata` is to be seen by the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad`.

Bhagavān said:

> If, again, Subhūti, the `Tathāgata` were to be seen by the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad`, a wheel-turning king would also be a `Tathāgata`.

He said:

> As I understand the meaning of the Bhagavān's words, the `Tathāgata` is not to be seen by the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad`.

Then, indeed, the Bhagavān at that time recited these verses:
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
tat kiṃ manyase subhūte api tv asti sa kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhaḥ āha | nāsti sa bhagavan kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhaḥ bhagavān āha | evam etat subhūte evam etat aṇur api tatra dharmo na saṃvidyate nopalabhyate tenocyate 'nuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir iti | api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte samaḥ sa dharmo na tatra kiṃcid viṣamas tenocyate 'nuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir iti | nirjīvatvena niḥsatvatvena niṣpudgalatvena samā sānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhiḥ sarvaiḥ kuśalair dharmair abhisaṃbudhyate | kuśalā dharmāḥ kuśalā dharmā iti subhūte adharmāś caiva te tathāgatena bhāṣitās tenocyante kuśalā dharmā iti | yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte yāvantas trisāhasramahāsāhasre lokadhātau sumeravaḥ parvatarājās tāvato rāśīn saptānāṃ ratnānām abhisaṃhṛtya dānaṃ dadyād yaś cetaḥ prajñāpāramitāyā antaśaś catuṣpadikām api gāthām udgṛhya parebhyo deśayed asya subhūte puṇyaskandhasyāsau pūrvakaḥ puṇyaskandhaḥ śatatamīm api kalān nopaiti | yāvad upaniśām

api na kṣamate | tat kiṃ manyase subhūte api nu tathāgatasyaivaṃ bhavati | mayā satvā mocitā iti | na khalu punaḥ subhūte-r-evaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ | tat kasya hetoḥ na sa kaścit satvo yas tathāgatena mocitaḥ yadi punaḥ subhūte kaścit satvo 'bhaviṣyad yas tathāgatena mocitaḥ sa eva tasyātmagrāho 'bhaviṣyat satvagrāho jīvagrāhaḥ pudgalagrāhaḥ ātmagrāha iti subhūte agrāha eṣa tathāgatena bhāṣitaḥ sa ca bālapṛthagjanair udgṛhītaḥ bālapṛthagjanā iti subhūte ajanā ete tathāgatena bhāṣitās tenocyaṃte bālapṛthagjanā iti | tat kiṃ manyase subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ āhaivam etad bhagaval lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ bhagavān āha | sacet punaḥ subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyo 'bhaviṣyad rājāpi cakravartī tathāgato 'bhaviṣyat* āha | yathāhaṃ bhagavato bhāṣitasyārtham ājānāmi | na lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ // atha khalu bhagavāṃs tasyāṃ velāyām imā gāthā abhāṣataḥ //
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
10.1 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte api tv asti sa kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhaḥ āha |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(tu) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(dharma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) 🚹⨀①(abhisambuddha) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
that | what | do you think | Subhūti | even | but | is there | that | any | dharma | which | by the thus-gone one | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | fully awakened | he said  
What do you think, Subhūti, is there any dharma which has been fully awakened to as unsurpassed perfect awakening by the thus-gone one?

10.2 nāsti sa bhagavan kaścid dharmo yas tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhaḥ bhagavān āha |  
⏏️(nāsti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(dharma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) 🚹⨀①(abhisambuddha) 🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
there is not | that | Bhagavān | any | dharma | which | by the thus-gone one | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | fully awakened | the Bhagavān | said  
There is no such dharma, Bhagavān, which has been fully awakened to as unsurpassed perfect awakening by the thus-gone one.

10.3 evam etat subhūte evam etat aṇur api tatra dharmo na saṃvidyate nopalabhyate tenocyate 'nuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir iti |  
⏏️(evam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(evam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀①(aṇu) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(tatra) 🚹⨀①(dharma) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(saṃvidyate) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upalambhyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) ⏏️(iti)  
thus | this | Subhūti | thus | this | even a minute | even | therein | dharma | not | exists | not | is found | therefore | is called | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | thus  
So it is, Subhūti, so it is; not even a minute dharma is found or perceived there, and therefore it is called 'unsurpassed perfect awakening'.

10.4 api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte samaḥ sa dharmo na tatra kiṃcid viṣamas tenocyate 'nuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir iti |  
⏏️(api) ⏏️(tu) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(sama) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(dharma) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(tatra) 🚹⨀①🆎(kim+cit) 🚹⨀①(viṣama) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) ⏏️(iti)  
but | indeed | furthermore | again | Subhūti | equal | that | dharma | not | there | anything | unequal | therefore | is called | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | thus  
But indeed, Subhūti, that dharma is equal; there is nothing unequal in it, and therefore it is called 'unsurpassed perfect awakening'.

10.5 nirjīvatvena niḥsatvatvena niṣpudgalatvena samā sānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhiḥ sarvaiḥ kuśalair dharmair abhisaṃbudhyate |  
🚻⨀③🔽(tva)🆎(nis+jīva) 🚻⨀③🔽(tva)🆎(nis+sattva) 🚻⨀③🔽(tva)🆎(nis+pudgala) 🚺⨀①(sama) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂③(sarva) 🚻⨂③(kuśala) 🚹⨂③(dharma) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(abhisaṃbudhyate)  
by the state of being without a life-force | by the state of being without a being | by the state of being without a person | equal | that | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | by all | good | dharmas | is fully awakened to  
That unsurpassed perfect awakening is equal, being without a life-force, without a sentient being, and without a person; it is fully awakened to by all good dharmas.

10.6 kuśalā dharmāḥ kuśalā dharmā iti subhūte adharmāś caiva te tathāgatena bhāṣitās tenocyante kuśalā dharmā iti |  
🚹⨂①(kuśala) 🚹⨂①(dharma) 🚹⨂①(kuśala) 🚹⨀①(dharman) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+dharma) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨂①(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨂①(kuśala) 🚹⨀①(dharman) ⏏️(iti)  
good dharmas | dharmas | good dharmas | dharmas | thus | Subhūti | non-dharmas | and | indeed | they | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | are called | good dharmas | dharmas | thus  
Subhūti, those which are called 'good dharmas' and 'good dharmas' are indeed spoken of by the thus-gone one as non-dharmas; therefore they are called 'good dharmas'.

10.7 yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte yāvantas trisāhasramahāsāhasre lokadhātau sumeravaḥ parvatarājās tāvato rāśīn saptānāṃ ratnānām abhisaṃhṛtya dānaṃ dadyād yaś cetaḥ prajñāpāramitāyā antaśaś catuṣpadikām api gāthām udgṛhya parebhyo deśayed asya subhūte puṇyaskandhasyāsau pūrvakaḥ puṇyaskandhaḥ śatatamīm api kalān nopaiti |  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚻⨁①(subhūta) 🚹⨂①(yāvat) 🚺⨁②🆎(tri+sāhasra+mahā+sāhasra) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(loka+dhātu) 🚹⨂①(sumeru) 🚹⨂①🆎(parvata+rāja) 🚹⨀⑤(tāvat) 🚹⨂②(rāśi) 🚻⨂⑥(saptan) 🚻⨂⑥(ratna) ⏏️(abhisaṃhṛtya) 🚻⨀①(dāna) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(dadāti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚻⨀①(cetas) 🚺⨀⑤🆎(prajñā+pāramitā) ⏏️🆎(anta+śas) 🚺⨀②🆎(catuṣ+padikā) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀②(gāthā) 🔽(ya)(udgṛhṇāti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂④(para) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(deśayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(idam) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(puṇya+skandha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(adas) 🚹⨀①(pūrvi) 🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🚺⨀②🆎(śata+tami) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨂②(kala) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(upaiti)  
who | and | indeed | furthermore | Subhūti | as many as | in the three-thousand-great-thousand | world system | Sumerus | kings of mountains | so many | heaps | of seven | jewels | having gathered together | gift | should give | who | from this | perfection of wisdom | even as little as | four-line | even | verse | having taken up | to others | should teach | of this | Subhūti | mass of merit | that | former | mass of merit | hundredth | even | part | not | approaches  
And furthermore, Subhūti, if someone were to gather together as many collections of the seven jewels as there are Sumeru kings of mountains in the three-thousand-great-thousand world system and give them as a gift, and if someone else were to take up even a single four-line verse from this `prajñāpāramitā` (perfection of wisdom) and teach it to others, then, Subhūti, that former mass of merit would not approach even a hundredth part of this mass of merit.

10.8 yāvad upaniśām  
⏏️(yāvat) 🚺⨀②(upaniśā)  
as far as | an analogy  
as far as an analogy

10.9 api na kṣamate |  
⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kṣamate)  
even | not | is able  
It does not bear even that.

10.10 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte api nu tathāgatasyaivaṃ bhavati |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nu) 🚹⨀⑥(tathāgata) ⏏️(evam) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati)  
that | what | you think | Subhūti | even | now | of the thus-gone one | thus | it occurs  
What do you think, Subhūti, does it occur to the thus-gone one in this way?

10.11 mayā satvā mocitā iti |  
*️⃣👆⚧⨀③(asmad) 🚹⨂①(sattva) 🔴⨀🤟⏩(mocyate) ⏏️(iti)  
by me | beings | liberated | thus  
I have liberated beings.

10.12 na khalu punaḥ subhūte-r-evaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ |  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(evam) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya)  
not | indeed | again | Subhūti's | thus | to be seen  
But, Subhūti, you should certainly not view it in this way.

10.13 tat kasya hetoḥ na sa kaścit satvo yas tathāgatena mocitaḥ yadi punaḥ subhūte kaścit satvo 'bhaviṣyad yas tathāgatena mocitaḥ sa eva tasyātmagrāho 'bhaviṣyat satvagrāho jīvagrāhaḥ pudgalagrāhaḥ ātmagrāha iti subhūte agrāha eṣa tathāgatena bhāṣitaḥ sa ca bālapṛthagjanair udgṛhītaḥ bālapṛthagjanā iti subhūte ajanā ete tathāgatena bhāṣitās tenocyaṃte bālapṛthagjanā iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(sattva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(mocita) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(sattva) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(mocita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(tad) 🚹⨀①🆎(ātma+grāha) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) 🚹⨀①🆎(sattva+grāha) 🚹⨀①🆎(jīva+grāha) 🚹⨀①🆎(pudgala+grāha) 🚹⨀①🆎(ātma+grāha) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+grāha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂③🆎(bāla+pṛthag+jana) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(udgṛhīta) 🚹⨂①🆎(bāla+pṛthag+jana) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨂①🆎(na+jana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(etad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨂①🆎(bāla+pṛthag+jana) ⏏️(iti)  
that | of what | cause | not | that | anyone | being | who | by the thus-gone one | liberated | if | again | Subhūti | anyone | being | had existed | who | by the thus-gone one | liberated | that | itself | of him | grasping at a self | would have been | grasping at a being | grasping at a life-force | grasping at a person | grasping at a self | thus | Subhūti | non-grasping | this | by the thus-gone one | spoken | that | and | by foolish ordinary people | grasped | foolish ordinary people | thus | Subhūti | non-people | these | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | are called | foolish ordinary people | thus  
For what reason? There is no being whatsoever who has been liberated by the thus-gone one. If, however, Subhūti, there were any being who had been liberated by the thus-gone one, that itself would be his grasping at a self, grasping at a being, grasping at a life-force, and grasping at a person. Subhūti, this so-called 'grasping at a self' has been spoken of by the thus-gone one as non-grasping, yet it is grasped by foolish ordinary people. Subhūti, these so-called 'foolish ordinary people' have been spoken of by the thus-gone one as non-people; therefore they are called 'foolish ordinary people'.

10.14 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ āhaivam etad bhagaval lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ bhagavān āha |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha) ⏏️(evam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) 🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
then | what | do you think | Subhūti | by the physical characteristics | the thus-gone one | should be seen | he said | thus | this | Bhagavān | by the physical characteristics | the thus-gone one | should be seen | the Bhagavān | said  
Then what do you think, Subhūti, should the thus-gone one be seen by the physical characteristics? He said: 'Yes, Bhagavān, he should be seen by the physical characteristics.' The Bhagavān said:

10.15 sacet punaḥ subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyo 'bhaviṣyad rājāpi cakravartī tathāgato 'bhaviṣyat āha |  
⏏️(sacet) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) 🚹⨀①(rājan) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀①(cakravartin) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
if | again | Subhūti | by the physical characteristics | the thus-gone one | should be seen | were to be | a king | even | a Cakravartin | the thus-gone one | were to be | he said  
If, however, Subhūti, the thus-gone one were to be seen by the physical characteristics, then even a universal monarch would be the thus-gone one.

10.16 yathāhaṃ bhagavato bhāṣitasyārtham ājānāmi |  
⏏️(yathā) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha) 🚹⨀⑥(bhagavat) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāṣita) 🚻⨀①(artha) 🟢⨀👆▶️(ājānāti)  
as | I say | of the Bhagavān | spoken | the meaning | I understand  
As I understand the meaning of what the Bhagavān has spoken,

10.17 na lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgato draṣṭavyaḥ // atha khalu bhagavāṃs tasyāṃ velāyām imā gāthā abhāṣataḥ //  
⏏️(na) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) ⏏️(atha) ⏏️(khalu) 🚹⨀①(bhagavat) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀⑦(tad) 🚺⨀⑦(velā) *️⃣🤟🚺⨂①(idam) 🚺⨀①(gāthā) 🔵⨀🤟↩️(bhāṣate)  
not | by the physical characteristics | the thus-gone one | to be seen | then | indeed | the Bhagavān | at that | time | these | verses | spoke  
The thus-gone one is not to be seen by the physical characteristics. Then, at that time, the Bhagavān spoke these verses.

### §§11

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
> Those who saw me by form, \
> Those who followed me by voice, \
> Engaged in false efforts, \
> Those people will not see me. \
> The `Buddha` (awakened one) is to be seen from the `dharma` (teaching), \
> The `dharmakāya` (dharma-body) is the `Tathāgata`. \
> And `dharmatā` (the nature of things) is unknowable; \
> It cannot be known.
>
> What do you think, Subhūti, was `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` (unexcelled perfect and complete awakening) fully awakened to by the `Tathāgata` through the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad` (perfection of marks)? Not so, indeed, Subhūti, should it be seen. Not, Subhūti, was `anuttarā samyaksaṃbodhi` fully awakened to by the `Tathāgata` through the `lakṣaṇasaṃpad`. That which, furthermore, Subhūti, might be thus: "For those who have set out in the `bodhisatvayāna` (`bodhisatvayāna`-way), the destruction or annihilation of any `dharma` (phenomenon) is proclaimed" — not so, indeed, Subhūti, should it be seen. For those who have set out in the `bodhisatvayāna`, the destruction or annihilation of any `dharma` is not proclaimed. And furthermore, Subhūti, if a `kulaputra` (son of good family) or `kuladuhitṛ` (daughter of good family), having filled `lokadhātus` (world-systems) equal to the sands of the river Ganges with the seven precious things, were to give a gift to the `Tathāgatas`, `Arhats`, `Samyaksaṃbuddhas`, and if a `bodhisattva` (awakening-being) should attain `kṣānti` (forbearance) with regard to selfless `dharmas`, this one would generate much more `puṇya` (merit) on that account. Not, indeed, Subhūti, should a `puṇyaskandha` (mass of merit) be grasped by a `bodhisattva`.

He said:

> The `puṇyaskandha`, Bhagavān, should be taken up.

Bhagavān said:

> It should be taken up, Subhūti, not grasped. Therefore it is said, 'it should be taken up'.
> 
> But furthermore, Subhūti, whoever should say thus: "The `Tathāgata` goes or comes, or stands, or sits, or lies down," he does not understand the meaning of my words. For what reason? '`Tathāgata`', Subhūti, has not come from anywhere, nor gone to anywhere. Therefore he is called '`Tathāgata`', `Arhat`, `Samyaksaṃbuddha`. And furthermore, Subhūti, if a `kulaputra` or `kuladuhitṛ` were to grind into ink as many `lokadhātus` (world-systems) as there are dust particles in the three-thousand-great-thousand `lokadhātu` — just as, for instance, a collection of atoms — what do you think, Subhūti, would that collection of atoms be large? He said, so it is, Bhagavān, that collection of atoms would be large. For what reason? If, Bhagavān, it were a collection, the Bhagavān would not have said, 'a collection of atoms'. For what reason? That which is called 'a collection of atoms' has been spoken of by the Bhagavān as a no-collection. Therefore it is called 'a collection of atoms'. And that which the `Tathāgata` speaks of as the 'three-thousand-great-thousand `lokadhātu`', that has been spoken of by the `Tathāgata` as a no-system. Therefore it is called the 'three-thousand-great-thousand `lokadhātu`'. For what reason? If, Bhagavān, a system existed, that itself, Bhagavān, would be a `piṇḍagrāha` (grasp of a substance). And that very thing which has been spoken of by the `Tathāgata` as a `piṇḍagrāha`, has been spoken of by the `Tathāgata` as a non-grasp. Therefore it is called '`piṇḍagrāha`'.
</TabItem>

tat kiṃ manyase subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhāḥ na khalu punaḥ subhūte evaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ na subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhā| yat khalu punaḥ subhūte syād evaṃ bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitaiḥ kasyacid dharmasya vināśaḥ prajñapta ucchedo vā na khalu punaḥ subhūte evaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ | na bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitaiḥ kasyacid dharmasya vināśaḥ prajñapto nocchedaḥ yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā gaṃgānadīvālukopamāl lokadhātūn saptaratnapratipūrṇān kṛtvā tathāgatebhyo 'rhadbhyaḥ samyaksaṃbuddhebhyo dānaṃ dadyād yaś ca bodhisatvo nirātmakeṣu dharmeṣu kṣāntiṃ pratilabheta | ayam eva tato bahutaraṃ puṇyaṃ prasaveta | na khalu punaḥ subhūte bodhisatvena puṇyaskandhaḥ parigrahītavyaḥ āha | puṇyaskandho bhagavan parigrahītavyaḥ bhagavān āha | parigrahītavyaḥ subhūte nodgrahītavyaḥ tenocyate parigrahītavyaḥ

api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte yaḥ kaścid evaṃ vadet tathāgato gacchati vāgacchati vā | tiṣṭhati vā niṣīdati vā śayyāṃ vā kalpayati | na me sa bhāṣitasyārtham ājānāti | tat kasya hetoḥ tathāgata iti subhūte na kutaścid āgato na kvacid gataḥ tenocyate tathāgato 'rhan samyaksaṃbuddha iti | yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā yāvantas trisāhasramahāsāhasre lokadhātau pṛthivīrajāṃsi tāvato lokadhātūn maṣiṃ kuryāt tadyathāpi nāma paramāṇusaṃcayas tat kiṃ manyase subhūte bahu sa paramāṇusaṃcayo bhavet āhaivam etad bhagavan bahu sa paramāṇusaṃcayo bhavet tat kasya hetoḥ saced bhagavan saṃcayo 'bhaviṣyan na bhagavān avakṣyat paramāṇusaṃcaya iti | tat kasya hetoḥ yo 'sau paramāṇusaṃcayo bhāṣitaḥ asaṃcayaḥ sa bhagavatā bhāṣitas tenocyate paramāṇusaṃcaya iti | yac ca tathāgato bhāṣati tṛsāhasramahāsāhasro lokadhātur iti | adhātuḥ sa tathāgatena bhāṣitas tenocyate trisāhasramahāsāhasro lokadhātur iti | tat kasya hetoḥ saced bhagavan dhātur abhaviṣyat sa eva bhagavan piṇḍagrāho 'bhaviṣyad yaś caiva tathāgatena piṇḍagrāho bhāṣitaḥ agrāhaḥ sa tathāgatena bhāṣitas tenocyate piṇḍagrāha iti |

11.1 ye māṃ rūpeṇa adrākṣur ye māṃ ghoṣeṇa anvayuḥ mithyāprahāṇaprasṛtā na māṃ drakṣyanti te janāḥ draṣṭavyo dharmato buddho dharmakāyas tathāgataḥ dharmatā cāpy avijñeyā na sā śakyaṃ vijānituṃ //  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(asmad) 🚻⨀③(rūpa) 🟢⨂🤟⏮(paśyati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(asmad) 🚹⨀③(ghoṣa) 🟢⨂🤟↩️(anuyāti) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(mithyā+prahā+ṇa+prasṛta) ⏏️(na) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(asmad) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(paśyati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①(jana) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) 🚹⨀⑤(dharma) 🚹⨀①(buddha) 🚹⨀①🆎(dharma+kāya) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①🔽(tā)(dharmatā) 🚹⨀①(cāpin) 🚺⨀①🔽(ya)🆎(na+vijñeya) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🔽(ya)(śakya) 🚹⨀①(vijāni)  
those who | me | by form | have seen | those who | me | by sound | have followed | those who have set out on a false path of abandonment | not | me | will see | those | people | is to be seen | by the Dharma | the Buddha | the Dharma-body | the thus-gone one | the true nature of things | and also | is unknowable | not | it | is possible | to be known  
Those who have seen me by my `rūpa` (form), or those who have followed me by my voice, are set out on a false path of abandonment and will not see me; the Buddha is to be seen through the Dharma, for the thus-gone one is the Dharma-body, and that true nature of things is unknowable and cannot be fully understood.

11.2 tat kiṃ manyase subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhāḥ na khalu punaḥ subhūte evaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ na subhūte lakṣaṇasaṃpadā tathāgatenānuttarā samyaksaṃbodhir abhisaṃbuddhā|  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(manyate) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) 🚹⨂①(abhisambuddha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(evam) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚺⨀③🆎(lakṣaṇa+sampad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(samyak+saṃbodhi) 🚺⨀①(abhisambuddha)  
then | what | do you think | Subhūti | by the physical characteristics | by the thus-gone one | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | fully awakened | not | indeed | furthermore | Subhūti | thus | to be seen | not | Subhūti | by the physical characteristics | by the thus-gone one | unsurpassed | perfect awakening | fully awakened  
What do you think, Subhūti, was the unsurpassed perfect awakening fully awakened by the thus-gone one through the physical characteristics? Subhūti, you should certainly not view it in that way; Subhūti, the unsurpassed perfect awakening was not fully awakened by the thus-gone one through the physical characteristics.

11.3 yat khalu punaḥ subhūte syād evaṃ bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitaiḥ kasyacid dharmasya vināśaḥ prajñapta ucchedo vā na khalu punaḥ subhūte evaṃ draṣṭavyaṃ |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) ⏏️(evam) 🚹⨂③🔽(ta)🆎(bodhi+sattva+yāna+saṃprasthita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kaścit) 🚹⨀⑥(dharma) 🚹⨀①(vināśa) 🚹⨀①(prajñapta) 🚻⨀⑤(ucchedi) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(evam) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(draṣṭavya)  
that | indeed | furthermore | Subhūti | should be | thus | by those who have set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas | of any | dharma | destruction | designated | or annihilation | or | not | indeed | furthermore | Subhūti | thus | should be seen  
Furthermore, Subhūti, if it should be thought that those who have set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas designate the destruction or annihilation of any dharma, Subhūti, you should certainly not view it in that way.

11.4 na bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitaiḥ kasyacid dharmasya vināśaḥ prajñapto nocchedaḥ yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā gaṃgānadīvālukopamāl lokadhātūn saptaratnapratipūrṇān kṛtvā tathāgatebhyo 'rhadbhyaḥ samyaksaṃbuddhebhyo dānaṃ dadyād yaś ca bodhisatvo nirātmakeṣu dharmeṣu kṣāntiṃ pratilabheta |

not | by those who have set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas | of any | dharma | destruction | designated | not | annihilation | whoever | and | indeed | furthermore | Subhūti | son of good family | or | daughter of good family | or | as numerous as the sands of the river Ganges | world systems | filled with the seven precious things | having made | to the thus-gone ones | to the liberated ones | to the Samyaksaṃbuddha | gift | should give | whoever | and | bodhisattva | in those that are without self | known, present | dharmas | patience | should attain  
not by those who have set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas is the destruction or annihilation of any dharma designated; furthermore, Subhūti, if a son of good family or a daughter of good family were to fill world systems as numerous as the sands of the river Ganges with the seven precious things and give them as a gift to the thus-gone, liberated, fully awakened ones, and if a bodhisattva were to attain patience in dharmas that are without self,

this | only | than that | more | merit | would produce  
This alone would produce more merit than that.

11.6 na khalu punaḥ subhūte bodhisatvena puṇyaskandhaḥ parigrahītavyaḥ āha |  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(khalu) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) 🚹⨀③(bodhisattva) 🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(parigrahītavya) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)

Not indeed, O Subhūti, should a bodhisattva grasp a mass of merit.

11.7 puṇyaskandho bhagavan parigrahītavyaḥ bhagavān āha |  
🚹⨀①🆎(puṇya+skandha) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(parigrahītavya) 🚹⨀①(bhagavat) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(āha)  
mass of merit | Bhagavān | to be grasped | the Bhagavān | said  
Bhagavān, the mass of merit is to be grasped.

11.8 parigrahītavyaḥ subhūte nodgrahītavyaḥ tenocyate parigrahītavyaḥ  
🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(parigrahītavya) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(udgrahītavya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨀①🔽(tavya)(parigrahītavya)  
to be grasped | O Subhūti | not | to be grasped | therefore | it is said | to be grasped  
O Subhūti, it is to be grasped, but it is not to be grasped; therefore it is said that it is to be grasped.

11.9 api tu khalu punaḥ subhūte yaḥ kaścid evaṃ vadet tathāgato gacchati vāgacchati vā |

But indeed, O Subhūti, if anyone should say that the thus-gone one goes or comes,

11.10 tiṣṭhati vā niṣīdati vā śayyāṃ vā kalpayati |  
🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati) ⏏️(vā) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(niṣīdati) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀②(śayyā) ⏏️(vā) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(kalpayati)  
stands | or | sits | or | a bed | or | lies down on  
He stands, or sits, or lies down.

11.11 na me sa bhāṣitasyārtham ājānāti |  
⏏️(na) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(asmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāṣita) 🚻⨀②(artha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(ājānāti)  
not | my | he | of the spoken | meaning | understands  
He does not understand the meaning of what I have spoken.

11.12 tat kasya hetoḥ tathāgata iti subhūte na kutaścid āgato na kvacid gataḥ tenocyate tathāgato 'rhan samyaksaṃbuddha iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀⓪(subhūti) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(kuta+cit) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(āgata) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(kvacit) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(gata) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①(arhat) 🚹⨀①(samyaksaṃbuddha) ⏏️(iti)  
that | of what | cause | thus-gone one | thus | Subhūti | not | from anywhere | come | not | anywhere | gone | therefore | is called | thus-gone one | liberated one | Samyaksaṃbuddha | thus  
For what reason? Subhūti, because the thus-gone one has not come from anywhere and has not gone anywhere, he is therefore called the thus-gone one, the liberated one, the Samyaksaṃbuddha.

11.13 yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā yāvantas trisāhasramahāsāhasre lokadhātau pṛthivīrajāṃsi tāvato lokadhātūn maṣiṃ kuryāt tadyathāpi nāma paramāṇusaṃcayas tat kiṃ manyase subhūte bahu sa paramāṇusaṃcayo bhavet āhaivam etad bhagavan bahu sa paramāṇusaṃcayo bhavet tat kasya hetoḥ saced bhagavan saṃcayo 'bhaviṣyan na bhagavān avakṣyat paramāṇusaṃcaya iti |

who | and | indeed | furthermore | Subhūti | son of a noble family | or | daughter of a noble family | or | as many as | in the three-thousand-great-thousand | world system | dust particles of the earth | that many | world systems | into ink | should make | just as | even | as if | a heap of atoms | that | what | do you think | Subhūti | much | that | heap of atoms | would be | he said | thus | this | Bhagavān | much | that | heap of atoms | would be | that | of what | reason | if | Bhagavān | heap | had existed | not | Bhagavān | would have spoken | heap of atoms | thus  
And furthermore, Subhūti, if a son of good family or a daughter of good family were to grind into ink as many world systems as there are dust particles of earth in the three-thousand-great-thousand world system, just like a heap of atoms—what do you think, Subhūti, would that heap of atoms be much? He said: 'It is so, Bhagavān, that heap of atoms would be much.' For what reason? If, Bhagavān, that heap had existed, the Bhagavān would not have called it a 'heap of atoms'.

11.14 tat kasya hetoḥ yo 'sau paramāṇusaṃcayo bhāṣitaḥ asaṃcayaḥ sa bhagavatā bhāṣitas tenocyate paramāṇusaṃcaya iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(adas) 🚹⨀①🆎(paramāṇu+saṃcaya) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+saṃcaya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀③(bhagavat) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨀①🆎(paramāṇu+saṃcaya) ⏏️(iti)  
that | of what | reason | which | this | heap of atoms | spoken | non-heap | that | by the Bhagavān | spoken | therefore | is called | heap of atoms | thus  
For what reason? That which was spoken of as a heap of atoms was spoken of by the Bhagavān as a non-heap; therefore it is called a heap of atoms.

11.15 yac ca tathāgato bhāṣati tṛsāhasramahāsāhasro lokadhātur iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhāṣati) 🚹⨀①🆎(tri+sāhasra+mahā+sāhasra) 🚹⨀①🆎(loka+dhātu) ⏏️(iti)  
which | and | the thus-gone one | speaks | the three-thousand-great-thousand | world system | thus  
And that which the thus-gone one speaks as 'the three-thousand-great-thousand world system'

11.16 adhātuḥ sa tathāgatena bhāṣitas tenocyate trisāhasramahāsāhasro lokadhātur iti |  
🚹⨀①🆎(na+dhātu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨀①🆎(tri+sāhasra+mahā+sāhasra) 🚹⨀①🆎(loka+dhātu) ⏏️(iti)  
non-component | that | by the thus-gone one | spoken | by that | is called | three-thousand-great-thousand | world system | thus  
That which is a non-component was spoken of by the thus-gone one; therefore it is called the three-thousand-great-thousand world system.

11.17 tat kasya hetoḥ saced bhagavan dhātur abhaviṣyat sa eva bhagavan piṇḍagrāho 'bhaviṣyad yaś caiva tathāgatena piṇḍagrāho bhāṣitaḥ agrāhaḥ sa tathāgatena bhāṣitas tenocyate piṇḍagrāha iti |  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀⑤(hetu) ⏏️(saced) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚹⨀①(dhātu) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⓪(bhagavat) 🚹⨀①🆎(piṇḍa+grāha) 🟢⨀🤟🔀(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🆎(piṇḍa+grāha) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+grāha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀③(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāṣita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate) 🚹⨀①🆎(piṇḍa+grāha) ⏏️(iti)  
that | of what | cause | if | Bhagavān | component | had been | that | very | Bhagavān | grasp of substance | had been | which | and | very | by the thus-gone one | grasp of substance | spoken | non-grasp | that | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | is called | grasp of substance | thus  
For what reason? If, Bhagavān, there were a `dhātu` (component), that would be a `piṇḍagrāha` (grasp of substance), Bhagavān; and that which the thus-gone one has spoken as a grasp of substance is a non-grasp, and therefore it is called a grasp of substance.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

### §§12

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
Bhagavān said:

> `Piṇḍagrāha` (grasp of a substance), Subhūti, is indeed a non-conventional, inexpressible `dharma` (phenomenon). It is grasped by `bālapṛthagjanas` (foolish common people). For what reason? Whoever, Subhūti, should say thus: "An `ātmadṛṣṭi` (view of a self), a `satvadṛṣṭi` (view of a being), a `jīvadṛṣṭi` (view of a life-principle), a `pudgaladṛṣṭi` (view of a person) has been spoken of by the `Tathāgata`," would he, Subhūti, be speaking correctly?

He said:

> No, Bhagavān. For what reason? That which, Bhagavān, is the `ātmadṛṣṭi` spoken of by the `Tathāgata`, has been spoken of by the `Tathāgata` as a non-view. Therefore it is called '`ātmadṛṣṭi`'.

Bhagavān said:

> Thus, Subhūti, by one who has set out in the `bodhisatvayāna` (`bodhisatvayāna`-way) are all `dharmas` (phenomena) to be known, to be convinced of. And they are to be convinced of in such a way that not even a `dharmasaṃjñā` (apperception of a phenomenon) arises. For what reason? '`Dharmasaṃjñā`, '`dharmasaṃjñā`', Subhūti, this is a no-apperception spoken of by the `Tathāgata`. Therefore it is called '`dharmasaṃjñā`'. And furthermore, Subhūti, if a `bodhisattva` (awakening-being), a `mahāsattva` (great being), having filled immeasurable, innumerable `lokadhātus` (world-systems) with the seven precious things, were to give a gift, and if a `kulaputra` (son of good family) or `kuladuhitṛ` (daughter of good family), having taken from this `Prajñāpāramitā` at least a four-lined verse, were to uphold it, teach it, master it, this one would on that account generate much more `puṇya` (merit), immeasurable, innumerable. And how should one proclaim it? As a no-proclamation one should proclaim it. Therefore it is said 'one should proclaim it'.
>
> A star, darkness, a lamp, \
> an illusion, dew, a bubble, \
> A dream, a flash of lightning, and a cloud, \
> Thus should the `saṃskṛta` (constructed) be seen.

This the Bhagavān spoke. The elder Subhūti, with a delighted mind, and those monks, nuns, laymen, and laywomen, and the world with its gods, humans, asuras, and gandharvas, rejoiced in what the Bhagavān had said.

**The Vajracchedikā Prajñāpāramitā is completed.**
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
bhagavān āha | piṇḍagrāhaś caivāvyavahāro 'nabhilāpyaḥ subhūte sa dharmaḥsa bālapṛthagjanair udgṛhītaḥ tat kasya hetoḥ yaḥ kaścit subhūte evaṃ vaded ātmadṛṣṭis tathāgatena bhāṣitā satvadṛṣṭir jīvadṛṣṭiḥ pudgaladṛṣṭiḥ api nu subhūte sa samyag vadan vadet āha | no bhagavaṃs tat kasya hetoḥ yā sā bhagavann ātmadṛṣṭis tathāgatena bhāṣitā adṛṣṭiḥ sā tathāgatena bhāṣitā tenocyate ātmadṛṣṭir iti | bhagavān āha | evaṃ subhūte bodhisatvayānasaṃprasthitena sarvadharmā jñātavyā adhimoktavyās tathā cādhimoktavyā yathā na dharmasaṃjñāpi pratyupatiṣṭhet tat kasya hetoḥ dharmasaṃjñā dharmasaṃjñeti subhūte asaṃjñaiṣā tathāgatena bhāṣitā tenocyate dharmasaṃjñeti | yaś ca khalu punaḥ subhūte bodhisatvo mahāsatvaḥ aprameyāsaṃkhyeyāl lokadhātūn saptaratnaparipūrṇān kṛtvā dānaṃ dadyād yaś ca kulaputro vā kuladuhitā vā itaḥ prajñāpāramitāyā antaśaś catuṣpadikām api gāthām udgṛhya dhārayed deśayet paryavāpnuyād ayam

eva tato bahutaraṃ puṇyaṃ prasavetāprameyam asaṃkhyeyaṃ | kaṭhaṃ ca saṃprakāśayed yathā na prakāśayet tenocyate saṃprakāśaye iti //

tārakā timiraṃ dīpo māyāvaśyāya budbudaḥ supinaṃ vidyud abhraṃ ca evaṃ draṣṭavya saṃskṛtaṃ //

idam avocad bhagavān āttamanā sthavirasubhūtis te ca bhikṣubhikṣuṇyupāsakopāsikāḥ sadevamānuṣāsuragandharvaś ca loko bhagavato bhāṣitam abhyanandan // //

 vajracchedikā prajñāpāramitā samāptāḥ // //

grasp of substance | and | indeed | not capable of discussion | inexpressible | Subhūti | that | dharma | it | by ordinary people | grasped | that | of what | reason | whoever | anyone | Subhūti | thus | should say | view of self | by the thus-gone one | spoken | view of a being | view of a life-force | view of a person | even | now | Subhūti | he | correctly | speaking | should say | said  
And that dharma, Subhūti, which is a grasp of substance, is not capable of discussion and is inexpressible; it is grasped by ordinary people. For what reason? If anyone, Subhūti, should say that the `ātmadṛṣṭi` (view of self) was spoken by the thus-gone one, or the view of a being, the view of a life-force, or the view of a person—would he, Subhūti, be speaking correctly?

12.3 no bhagavaṃs tat kasya hetoḥ yā sā bhagavann ātmadṛṣṭis tathāgatena bhāṣitā adṛṣṭiḥ sā tathāgatena bhāṣitā tenocyate ātmadṛṣṭir iti |

not | Bhagavān | that | of what | reason | which | that | Bhagavān | view of self | by the thus-gone one | spoken | non-view | that | by the thus-gone one | spoken | therefore | is called | view of self | thus  
No, Bhagavān. For what reason? That which the thus-gone one called a view of self, Bhagavān, was spoken of as a non-view by the thus-gone one; therefore it is called a view of self.

thus | Subhūti | by one who has set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas | all dharmas | are to be known | are to be resolved upon | and in that way | and | are to be resolved upon | so that | not | the apperception of dharmas | even | should arise | that | of what | reason | the apperception of dharmas | the apperception of dharmas | thus | Subhūti | a non-apperception | this | by the thus-gone one | was spoken | therefore | it is called | the apperception of dharmas | thus  
In this way, Subhūti, all dharmas are to be known and resolved upon by one who has set out in the vehicle of the bodhisattvas, and they are to be resolved upon in such a way that even the apperception of dharmas does not arise. For what reason? Subhūti, that which is called the apperception of dharmas is spoken of by the thus-gone one as a non-apperception; therefore it is called the apperception of dharmas.

and whoever, O Subhūti, a bodhisattva great being should give as a gift immeasurable and innumerable world systems filled with the seven precious things, and whoever, whether a son of good family or a daughter of good family, should take up from this perfection of wisdom even as little as a four-line verse and bear it in mind, teach it, and master it—this

Thus, he would produce more merit than that, immeasurable and innumerable.

12.9 tārakā timiraṃ dīpo māyāvaśyāya budbudaḥ supinaṃ vidyud abhraṃ ca evaṃ draṣṭavya saṃskṛtaṃ //

A star, darkness, a lamp, an illusion, dew, a bubble, a dream, lightning, and a cloud: in this way should the conditioned be viewed.

12.10 idam avocad bhagavān āttamanā sthavirasubhūtis te ca bhikṣubhikṣuṇyupāsakopāsikāḥ sadevamānuṣāsuragandharvaś ca loko bhagavato bhāṣitam abhyanandan // //

this | said | the Bhagavān | with a delighted mind | the elder Subhūti | they | and | the monks, nuns, laymen and laywomen | with its gods, humans, asuras and gandharvas | and | the world | of the Bhagavān | the speech | rejoiced  
The Bhagavān said this, and the elder Subhūti, with a delighted mind, as well as those monks, nuns, laymen, and laywomen, and the world of gods, humans, asuras, and gandharvas, rejoiced in what the Bhagavān had spoken.

12.11 vajracchedikā prajñāpāramitā samāptāḥ // //

The Diamond-Cutter Perfection of Wisdom is finished.
